Implemented in [taskActionGeometry](./analysis/taskActionGeometry/README.md). Your existing analysis call works.

- **All LDA axes:** five-fold CV repeated five times; held-out projections and variance metrics plotted as mean ± SEM across repeat means.
- **Directions:** sign-aligned average directions saved in `geometry.meanWeights`; unit-normalized versions in `geometry.weights`.
- **Time courses:** mean single-neuron decoding accuracy for the top 10% and 25%.
- **New summary:** x-axis = top 5%, 10%, …, 100% of neurons; two curves = population accuracy and mean single-neuron accuracy.
- **Choice:** training-selected three-frame window. **Task:** full-window average.

Neuron ranking and window selection use **inner training-only CV**, keeping test trials untouched.

These are already the defaults:
```matlab
cfg.cvFolds = 5;
cfg.cvRepeats = 5;
cfg.decoderTopFractions = [0.10 0.25];
cfg.decoderFractions = 0.05:0.05:1;
cfg.decoderWindowFrames = 3;
```

Detailed results are in `geometry.crossValidation` and `geometry.decoding`. CV requires at least **10 trials per condition per animal**; exclusions appear in `animalAudit`.

Validated with **20 synthetic tests**, including leakage checks and chance-level decoding, and inspected exported figures. Your example script was left unchanged.

> what is cross validation method? for each neuron, is it taking a split within that animal

Yes—**trials are split separately within each animal and each trial condition, but all neurons from that animal share the same split.**

For each of the five folds:

1. Within each **animal × condition**, approximately **80% of trials train** the model and **20% test** it.
2. **Every neuron and every time point from a trial stay together.** Neurons are not split independently, so within-animal trial correlations are preserved.
3. Neurons from all animals are pooled to fit the population LDA axis using only their respective training trials.
4. That axis is evaluated on the held-out trials.

For example:

| Animal | Training trials | Test trials |
|---|---|---|
| Animal A | A’s selected 80% | A’s remaining 20% |
| Animal B | B’s selected 80% | B’s remaining 20% |

Animal A and B have independent trial assignments because their trials are different recordings. For **population decoding**, held-out trials are independently sampled across animals to create pseudo-trials. Single-neuron decoding uses each neuron’s actual held-out trials.

The five folds are repeated with **five different random partitions**. Each trial is tested once per repeat.

There is also **three-fold inner CV within the training trials** to choose the top neurons and the best choice-decoding window.

Thus, this measures **generalization to unseen trials from the same animals**, not unseen animals or sessions.

>         if isempty(train{c,a}), continue; end
>         N=size(train{c,a},3);
>         assert(N>=J&&N-ceil(N/J)>=2,'neuralGeometry:InnerTrials','Insufficient training trials for inner folds.');
>         order=randperm(stream,N); z=zeros(N,1); z(order)=mod(0:N-1,J)+1; innerFold{c,a}=z;
>     end
> end
> validation=zeros(n,nt,nf);
> for j=1:J
>     tr=train; va=train;
>     for a=1:na
>         for c=1:nc
>             if isempty(train{c,a}), continue; end
>             keep=innerFold{c,a}~=j;
>             tr{c,a}=train{c,a}(:,:,keep); va{c,a}=train{c,a}(:,:,~keep);

> function m=singleModel(X,T,cfg,model,windows)
> n=height(model.neurons); nf=numel(model.variables); [nc,na]=size(X); nt=numel(model.time);
> m.offset=zeros(n,1); m.scale=ones(n,1); timeMean=zeros(n,nt,nc); timeVar=timeMean;
> for a=1:na
>     ix=find(model.neurons.animalIndex==a); if isempty(ix), continue; end
>     sums=zeros(numel(ix),1); squares=sums; N=0;
>     for c=1:nc
>         z=reshape(X{c,a},numel(ix),[]); sums=sums+sum(z,2); squares=squares+sum(z.^2,2); N=N+size(z,2);
>     end
>     switch cfg.normalization
>         case 'zscore'
>             m.offset(ix)=sums/N; m.scale(ix)=sqrt(max(0,(squares-sums.^2/N)/(N-1)));
>         case 'rms'
>             m.scale(ix)=sqrt(squares/N);
>     end
>     m.scale(m.scale==0)=1;
>     for c=1:nc
>         X{c,a}=(X{c,a}-m.offset(ix))./m.scale(ix);
>         timeMean(ix,:,c)=mean(X{c,a},3); timeVar(ix,:,c)=var(X{c,a},1,3);
>     end
> end
> m.w=zeros(n,nf); m.mid=m.w; m.valid=false(1,nf);
> m.wTime=zeros(n,nt,nf); m.midTime=m.wTime;
> for f=1:nf
>     levels=model.factors(f).levels; if numel(levels)~=2, continue; end
>     g=string(T.(model.variables(f))); win=windows{f};
>     M=reshape(mean(timeMean(:,win,:),2),n,nc);
>     % Total variance pools both within-trial time changes and across-trial noise.
>     V=reshape(mean(timeVar(:,win,:)+(timeMean(:,win,:)-reshape(M,n,1,nc)).^2,2),n,nc);
>     mu=zeros(n,2); noise=zeros(n,1); muTime=zeros(n,nt,2); noiseTime=zeros(n,nt);
>     for l=1:2
>         rows=find(g==levels(l)); mu(:,l)=mean(M(:,rows),2);
>         noise=noise+mean(V(:,rows)+(M(:,rows)-mu(:,l)).^2,2)/2;
>         muTime(:,:,l)=mean(timeMean(:,:,rows),3);
>         noiseTime=noiseTime+mean(timeVar(:,:,rows)+(timeMean(:,:,rows)-muTime(:,:,l)).^2,3)/2;
>     end
>     base=mean(noise); if base<=0, base=1; end
>     m.w(:,f)=(mu(:,2)-mu(:,1))./(noise+cfg.ridge*base);
>     m.mid(:,f)=mean(mu,2); m.valid(f)=true;
>     baseTime=mean(noiseTime,1); baseTime(baseTime<=0)=1;
>     m.wTime(:,:,f)=(muTime(:,:,2)-muTime(:,:,1))./(noiseTime+cfg.ridge*baseTime);
>     m.midTime(:,:,f)=mean(muTime,3);
> end
> end
>
> function acc=singleAccuracy(X,T,m,model,timeSpecific)
> [nc,na]=size(X); n=height(model.neurons); nt=numel(model.time); nf=numel(model.variables);
> acc=zeros(n,nt,nf);
> for f=1:nf
>     if ~m.valid(f), acc(:,:,f)=NaN; continue; end
>     levels=model.factors(f).levels; g=string(T.(model.variables(f)));
>     for a=1:na
>         ix=find(model.neurons.animalIndex==a); if isempty(ix), continue; end
>         if timeSpecific
>             mid=m.midTime(ix,:,f); w=m.wTime(ix,:,f);
>         else
>             mid=m.mid(ix,f); w=m.w(ix,f);
>         end
>         for c=1:nc
>             z=(X{c,a}-m.offset(ix))./m.scale(ix);
>             margin=(z-mid).*w;
>             if g(c)~=levels(2), margin=-margin; end
>             correct=double(margin>0)+0.5*double(margin==0);
>             acc(ix,:,f)=acc(ix,:,f)+mean(correct,3)/(2*sum(g==g(c)));
>         end
>     end
> end
> end
> ''')

> - Ran ````@'
> from pathlib import Path
> p=Path('analysis/taskActionGeometry/README.md');s=p.read_text()
> s=s.replace("cfg.decoderFractions=0.05:0.05:1; % Summary x-axis: top percent of neurons.\n",'')
> s=s.replace("cfg.decoderWindowFrames=3; % Choice summary; task uses the whole window.","cfg.decoderWindowFrames=3; % Fixed -3:-1 at 15 Hz; input must be choice-aligned dffChoice.")
> s=s.replace('not an animal-replicate statistical test. Only the new population decoder samples\nindependent test pseudo-trials across animals, with source indices recorded.', 'not an animal-replicate statistical test. Decoding uses each neuron\'s own real\nheld-out trials: there is no pooled population decoder or pseudo-trial sampling.')
> a=s.index('### First-axis and single-neuron linear decoders');b=s.index('## Axis fitting: math and implementation',a)
> s=s[:a]+'''### Real-trial single-neuron decoding
>
> The population decoder has been removed. Every accuracy is evaluated on that
> neuron's **actual held-out trials from its own animal**, with the same five-fold
> partitions used by geometry. No trials are paired or sampled across animals.
> Binary variables are supported; multilevel variables retain geometry but skip
> decoding. Neuron identities/order must still match across conditions within each
> animal.
>
> **Choice (`decoderChoiceVariable='action'`):** the input must be choice-aligned
> `dffChoice`, with time zero at choice threshold. Fit the scalar neuron classifier
> using only frames **-3, -2, -1**, and summarize its test accuracy only on these
> frames. At 15 Hz the sample times are -200, -133.33 and -66.67 ms; frame zero and
> all post-choice frames are excluded. All three frames must be present in
> `cfg.timeWindow`. The code checks the time grid and errors on incompatible
> alignment/rates instead of silently choosing a different window. There is no
> search for a best window. Stimulus-aligned data cannot be used as if its zero were
> choice; realign it before calling this analysis.
>
> **Task and other binary variables:** fit using all selected time points, and
> average the test accuracies across the full selected window. For both choice and
> task, the summary averages per-frame accuracies, **not trial responses before
> classification**. Geometry axis fitting is unchanged: it still uses the full
> selected time window; choice single-neuron coefficients now use the narrower
> pre-choice window and need not match those geometry coefficients.
>
> With equal class priors, single-neuron LDA has
>
> $$w_j=(\\mu_{2j}-\\mu_{1j})/(s_j^2+\\lambda),\\qquad
> h_j=(\\mu_{1j}+\\mu_{2j})/2.$$
>
> Classify as level 2 when $w_j(x_j-h_j)>0$. The means and variances pool training
> trials and the variable's fitting frames, balancing classes and conditions
> within classes. Zero-score ties count as 0.5. Test accuracy similarly balances
> classes and their subconditions. Normalization parameters are learned only from
> training trials; positive affine single-neuron normalization does not change
> these scalar decisions.
>
> The `decodingNeurons` figure plots **one point per neuron**, ranked by its own
> mean held-out accuracy, with SEM across repeat means and color indicating its
> animal. This final sorting is descriptive: it is not independent evidence that
> the best displayed neuron will perform as well on a new dataset. No averaging
> across neurons is done in this figure.
>
> The existing `decodingTime` figure still plots mean single-neuron accuracy for
> `decoderTopFractions=[0.10 0.25]`. For these groups, `decoderInnerFolds=3` ranks
> neurons using inner-training CV only: pre-choice accuracy for choice, whole-window
> accuracy for task. The resulting fixed neuron group is evaluated across the outer
> test time course. The choice classifier's fixed pre-choice threshold is also
> applied at other times to show temporal generalization; the **choice summary**
> only uses pre-choice frames. This top-group plot and its ranking are separate
> from the descriptive sorting in the individual-neuron plot.
>
> ### Investigating low accuracy despite selective mean traces
>
> The previous implementation fitted one decision threshold over the entire time
> window for every variable, and ranked choice neurons by whole-window accuracy.
> A short-lived pre-choice response could therefore be diluted by baseline and
> other periods. Choice fitting/ranking now use only the fixed pre-choice frames.
>
> A second issue can persist, especially for task: the baseline of both class
> responses may change strongly over time. One scalar threshold pooled across time
> can put *both* classes on the same side at a given time even when their means
> are clearly separated there. To diagnose this, every split also fits a separate
> single-neuron threshold/direction **at each time bin using training trials only**:
>
> - `decoding.mean.neuronTime`: fixed-window classifier, neuron x time x variable.
> - `decoding.mean.neuronSummary`: each neuron's accuracy in the stated summary window.
> - `decoding.mean.timeResolvedNeuronTime` / `timeResolvedNeuronSummary`: held-out
>   diagnostic with per-time-bin fits. A substantial increase indicates a cost of
>   using one fixed threshold/direction across time. This diagnostic does not replace
>   the main fixed-window metric or change the geometry axes.
>
> Synthetic tests reproduce 50% fixed-threshold versus near-perfect time-specific
> accuracy with a strong shared temporal baseline and perfectly separated classes.
> That establishes a possible mechanism, not the cause of an individual animal's
> scores. Trial-averaged traces also conceal trial-to-trial overlap. Finally, a
> neuron selective for S1 alone may distinguish L/R in T1 but not in T2; averaging
> across both tasks can weaken its action accuracy, and opposite preferences can
> cancel. Thus visible condition selectivity is not necessarily robust marginal
> choice/task decoding.
>
> ```matlab
> geometry.decoding.neuronTable  % Animal, original neuron row, accuracy, SEM, rank,
>                               % and timeResolvedAccuracy for the same cell.
> geometry.decoding.mean.neuronSummary       % neuron x variable
> geometry.decoding.mean.neuronTime          % neuron x time x variable
> geometry.decoding.displayOrder             % neuron indices sorted for each variable
> ```
>
> `crossValidation.assignments{condition,animal}` is original trial x repeat;
> entries give the held-out fold. `crossValidation.models{s}` stores training
> weights, scaling and axis labels. `crossValidation.splits{s}` stores held-out
> projection/variance/factor metrics. `crossValidation.varianceSEM` and `factorSEM`
> contain plotted repeat SEMs. `decoding.splits{s}` stores actual test `sourceTrial`
> indices, training ranks, thresholds, fit/summary window indices, and per-neuron
> accuracies. `decoding.mean` and `.sem` aggregate these over repeats.
> `decoderFractions` and `decoderPseudoTrials` are accepted but ignored for
> compatibility with saved configuration structures; no population curves are made.
> Old saved results lack held-out accuracies for individual neurons: rerun analysis
> rather than trying to recover them from old top-group averages.
>
> Training-only top-group selection follows
> [nested CV](https://scikit-learn.org/stable/auto_examples/model_selection/plot_nested_cross_validation_iris.html).
>
> ''' +s[b:]
> s=s.replace('| `decodingFraction` | Population and mean single-neuron accuracy versus top 5%, 10%, ..., 100%; choice uses a training-selected three-frame window, task the whole window. |', '| `decodingNeurons` | Individual neurons ranked by held-out accuracy, colored by animal, with repeat SEM. Choice uses fixed pre-choice frames -3:-1; task uses the full window. |')
> p.write_text(s)

> </details>
>
> The revised decoder passes the tests: it uses every neuron’s real held-out trials, and the choice summary excludes time zero and all later frames.
>
> A diagnostic test also reproduced the low-accuracy issue: strongly separated class responses can score 50% with one time-pooled threshold when both responses shift over time. Training a separate threshold at each time bin recovered the separation in that test. I’ve saved both scores for each neuron so you can check whether this explains your data; the synthetic result alone doesn’t establish that it does.

Updated [taskActionGeometry](./analysis/taskActionGeometry/README.md):

- Removed population decoding.
- Added **one point per neuron**, ranked by its cross-validated accuracy, colored by animal, with SEM.
- Choice decoding now fits and scores **frames −3, −2, −1** before threshold. At 15 Hz these are −200, −133, −67 ms; **frame zero is excluded**. Use `dffChoice` input.
- Task decoding retains the full analysis window.

A likely contributor to low scores was the **whole-window threshold and ranking**, which can dilute brief selectivity. > I found several concrete issues in `fn_runDecoder`: it only samples the first `min(nL,nR)` trials from each class, its shuffle can omit part of the larger class, and its weight-correlation calculation does not mask the diagonal correctly. The single-cell rule is essentially equal-prior LDA, but its variance and tie handling need safeguards.
>
> The old population rule sums single-cell LDA scores, so it ignores correlations between neurons. Poor population accuracy alone doesn’t prove the LDA assumptions are wrong. I’ll use a standardized linear SVM per animal, refit it for each top-neuron subset, and evaluate everything on held-out real trials. For this new analysis, each trial’s feature will be the mean activity in the requested frames.
>

> function z=runFold(train,test,o,stream)
> n=size(train{1},2); Q=numel(o.TopFractions);
> % Each inner training observation is predicted once, with class-specific counts.
> J=min(o.InnerFolds,min(cellfun(@(x)size(x,1),train)));

> function [w,mid]=lda(A,ridge)
> mu1=mean(A{1},1)'; mu2=mean(A{2},1)';
> variance=(var(A{1},0,1)'+var(A{2},0,1)')/2;
> base=max(mean(variance),eps); w=(mu1-mu2)./(variance+ridge*base);

> does my fn_rundecoder math work good?

<details><summary>2 previous messages</summary>

> I’ll compare your original decoder with the corrected version so I can distinguish the LDA math from the cross-validation issues.
>
> <details><summary>Ran a command</summary>
>
> - Ran `Get-Content "$env:TEMP\taskActionDecoderUpdate\fn_runDecoder_before.m"; Get-Content 'C:\Users\zzhu34\Documents\GitHub\ziyiFunction\analysis\fn_runDecoder.m' | Select-String -Pattern 'function .*fit|mu1|mu2|variance|lambda|mid=|w=|fitcsvm|score' -Context 2,3`
>
> </details>
</details>

**Your original single-neuron LDA math was correct for equal class priors and a shared variance.** The bigger problems were trial sampling, numerical edge cases, and how the population decoder combined neurons.

For neuron \(j\), your code computes

\[
w_j=\frac{\mu_{1j}-\mu_{2j}}{(\sigma_{1j}^{2}+\sigma_{2j}^{2})/2},
\qquad
c_j=w_j\frac{\mu_{1j}+\mu_{2j}}{2}.
\]

It predicts class 1 when \(w_jx_j>c_j\). This is the standard one-dimensional LDA decision rule.

An important consequence: **for a single neuron, the variance denominator does not change the classification boundary**, provided it is positive. Your rule is equivalent to

\[
(\mu_{1j}-\mu_{2j})
\left(x_j-\frac{\mu_{1j}+\mu_{2j}}{2}\right)>0.
\]

The boundary is halfway between the training class means; the mean difference determines its direction. Non-Gaussian responses do not make this computation invalid, although this boundary may not be optimal.

Your original **population** decoder used

\[
s(\mathbf x)=\sum_j w_j
\left(x_j-\frac{\mu_{1j}+\mu_{2j}}{2}\right).
\]

That is **diagonal-covariance LDA**: it ignores correlations between neurons. Full-covariance LDA instead uses

\[
\mathbf w=\Sigma_{\mathrm{within}}^{-1}
(\boldsymbol\mu_1-\boldsymbol\mu_2).
\]

Consequently, your population decoder could count correlated signals repeatedly or give unstable, low-variance neurons excessive weight. It was a valid simplified model, but it could perform worse than a strong individual neuron.

The implementation issues I corrected were:

- Unequal trial counts: only the first `min(N1,N2)` trials of each class were eligible.
- Zero variance: could produce infinite or undefined weights.
- Exact decision-boundary ties: were counted as incorrect for both classes.
- Shuffled controls: sampled the wrong subset when class sizes differed.

Your original repeated train/test splitting was a legitimate validation approach; it was **not inherently wrong because it wasn’t five-fold CV**. The sampling bug was the main issue. The revised function retains scalar LDA for individual neurons and uses training-ranked, cross-validated SVMs for populations.

> did you fix sampling and cross validation

Yes—both are fixed in [fn_runDecoder.m](~/Documents/GitHub/ziyiFunction/analysis/fn_runDecoder.m).

- **Sampling:** uses all trials from both classes, even with unequal counts.
- **Cross-validation:** stratified five-fold CV, repeated five times by default. Every trial is tested exactly once per repeat.
- **Population trials:** all neurons from one animal use the same trial splits.
- **Neuron selection:** top-performing neurons are ranked using inner CV on training trials only; outer test trials remain untouched.
- **Unequal counts:** accuracy is the average of the two class-specific accuracies, so the larger class cannot dominate.

Tests passed for unequal trial counts and for preventing held-out data from influencing neuron selection.

Use the registered **`data.bin` and `ops.npy`** directly. This skips TIFF/H5 conversion and motion registration, then runs ROI detection, trace extraction, and deconvolution.

This uses the **`ops`-based Suite2p API** used by your existing scripts; run it in the same Suite2p environment used for registration. [Suite2p implementation](https://github.com/MouseLand/suite2p/blob/v0.14.6/suite2p/run_s2p.py)

```python
from pathlib import Path
import numpy as np
from suite2p.run_s2p import run_plane

def detect_registered_rois(suite2p_folder):
    """Input: a suite2p folder, or a single plane0 folder."""
    folder = Path(suite2p_folder).expanduser().resolve()

    planes = (
        [folder] if (folder / "ops.npy").is_file()
        else sorted(folder.glob("plane[0-9]*"))
    )
    if not planes:
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"No plane folders found in {folder}")

    for plane in planes:
        ops_file = plane / "ops.npy"
        binary_file = plane / "data.bin"

        if not ops_file.is_file() or not binary_file.is_file():
            raise FileNotFoundError(
                f"{plane} must contain ops.npy and registered data.bin"
            )

        ops = np.load(ops_file, allow_pickle=True).item()

        # Update paths in case the registered folder was copied.
        ops["reg_file"] = str(binary_file)
        ops["save_path"] = str(plane)
        ops["ops_path"] = str(ops_file)

        if ops.get("nchannels", 1) == 2:
            chan2 = plane / "data_chan2.bin"
            if not chan2.is_file():
                raise FileNotFoundError(chan2)
            ops["reg_file_chan2"] = str(chan2)

        # Reuse registration; detect ROIs and extract traces.
        ops["do_registration"] = 0
        ops["do_regmetrics"] = False
        ops["roidetect"] = True
        ops["spikedetect"] = True
        ops["save_mat"] = True       # Also produce Fall.mat.

        # Preserve registered binaries; do not open raw movies.
        ops["keep_movie_raw"] = False
        ops["delete_bin"] = False
        ops["move_bin"] = False

        # Other parameters, including fs/tau, remain from saved ops.
        # ops["threshold_scaling"] = 1.0  # Optional detection threshold.

        print(f"Detecting ROIs and extracting traces: {plane}")
        run_plane(ops, ops_path=str(ops_file))

# Change this path:
detect_registered_rois(
    r"F:\zz180_PPC\imagingSession\zz180_20260302_2AFC1_parsed\suite2p"
)
```

Results are saved inside each `plane*` folder:

- `F.npy`: ROI fluorescence.
- `Fneu.npy`: neuropil fluorescence.
- `spks.npy`: deconvolved activity.
- `stat.npy`, `iscell.npy`: ROI definitions and classifications.
- `Fall.mat`: MATLAB output.

**Existing ROI/trace results and `ops.npy` will be overwritten.** The registered binary is retained.

> hwo should i revise path if py file is in the same folder

Since your Python script is beside the `suite2p` folder, replace the final call with:

```python
detect_registered_rois(
    Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "suite2p"
)
```

This finds `suite2p` relative to the script’s location, regardless of where you launch Python.

It expects `suite2p/plane0/ops.npy` and `suite2p/plane0/data.bin`.
